# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented here. Format loosely follows
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/).

---

## [v1.0.0] — Aether

First release under the **Aether** name. This is the same engine that shipped as
Mark XL v7 (memory system, agent planner, OSINT toolkit, plugin system, dashboard,
etc.) — this release is the rebrand + UI overhaul that turns it into a coherent,
presentable v1.0 rather than a new feature drop. If you're upgrading from a
Mark XL checkout, your `config/api_keys.json` and memory DB are untouched and
will keep working as-is.

### Changed — UI redesign
- Replaced the old blue/orange "targeting HUD" look with a new ethereal
  cyan/purple sci-fi theme (deep space black background, `#00f5ff` primary,
  `#9d4edd` secondary).
- Replaced `HudCanvas` (crosshair/reticle style) with `AetherOrb` — a glowing
  sphere with distinct animated states: idle breathing, listening pulse rings,
  thinking orbit dots, speaking audio bars + light waves, and a red→orange
  error flash.
- Bundled the Rajdhani font (OFL-licensed) for headings/display text, with a
  system-sans fallback for body text.
- Rebranded all in-app strings: window title, header, footer, status badges.
  Removed leftover "J.A.R.V.I.S" and "FatihMakes Industries" references.

### Added
- `Ctrl+Alt+A` — show/hide the window from anywhere.
- `Esc` — collapse the HUD down to just the orb (press again to restore full
  layout). Distinct from `F11` fullscreen.
- `docs/screenshots/` gallery + README screenshot section.
- `CHANGELOG.md` (this file) and `TESTING.md` manual test checklist.
- `version.py` — single-source `__version__`/`APP_NAME` constant, wired into
  the window title, header badge, `/help`, and the diagnostics report.
- Orb now shows a distinct **PROCESSING** state (already existed in the
  widget, wasn't being used) while a tool is actually executing, separate
  from **THINKING** during LLM generation — clearer visual feedback for
  "Aether is doing something" vs. "Aether is composing a reply."
- Tool activity-log lines now include a short argument hint, e.g.
  `SYS: ▶ web_search (query=weather in...)` instead of just the tool name.
- `detect_system_theme()` — best-effort OS light/dark detection (Windows
  registry check + Qt 6.5+ `styleHints().colorScheme()`), logged at startup.
  Aether's HUD stays dark by design either way; this is groundwork for the
  "proper theme switching" item on the roadmap, not a light-mode palette yet.

### Fixed — leftover branding sweep
- OS notifications from the `reminder` tool ("J.A.R.V.I.S Reminder" toast /
  banner / AppleScript / Windows Task Scheduler description) → "Aether".
- YouTube tool's URL-input dialog title ("J.A.R.V.I.S") → "Aether".
- Global hotkey default changed from `Ctrl+Alt+J` to `Ctrl+Alt+A` in
  `core/hotkey_manager.py` (this is the OS-level `keyboard`-package hotkey —
  separate from the in-window `QShortcut` added in the UI redesign, which
  was already on `Ctrl+Alt+A` but the two had drifted out of sync).
  Startup log line now reports whatever hotkey is actually bound instead of
  a hardcoded string.
- `/help` chat command's hotkey hint corrected from `Ctrl+Alt+J` to
  `Ctrl+Alt+A` and now includes `Esc collapse`.
- `core/diagnostics.py` status report header dropped a stale "v3" label in
  favor of the real `version.py` version string.
- No remaining `J.A.R.V.I.S` / `JARVIS` strings anywhere in `.py` files
  (verified with a repo-wide grep). "Mark-XLVIII" mentions in code comments
  (`main.py`, `dashboard/server.py`, `requirements.txt`,
  `config.example.json`) were left as-is — those are accurate historical
  attribution for a *different*, separate predecessor project the dashboard
  code was ported from, not leftover Aether branding.

### Fixed
- N/A this release — purely cosmetic/rebrand changes. See `IMPROVEMENTS_V6.md`
  and earlier for the bug-fix history from the Mark XL line.

### Known gaps / not yet done
- Left/right panel proportions still follow the original layout (compact
  system-monitor sidebar + wider conversation/controls panel) rather than a
  strict 40% conversation / 25% controls split — functional but not a full
  structural reflow.
- Settings/Configure panel still uses its original single-modal layout rather
  than the tabbed (Core / Appearance / Behavior / Shortcuts) design from the
  original spec.
- No wake-word support yet ("Hey Aether") — see roadmap.
- Orb is still QPainter/QWidget-based, not QGraphicsView + cached pixmaps —
  fine on modern hardware in testing, but flagged as a perf risk on
  lower-end machines per the roadmap; not yet profiled.
- No fade in/out on window show/hide, no config validation/migration, no
  "reset to defaults," no window position/size persistence yet.
- Screenshots in the gallery are headless placeholder renders (no live
  model/system data) — replace with real captures before publishing.

---

## Pre-rebrand history (Mark XL / JARVIS lineage)

Condensed from `IMPROVEMENTS.md` → `IMPROVEMENTS_V6.md`. Kept here for
reference; those files remain in the repo with full detail.

- **v7 / Mark XL** — Multi-panel UI, agent planning loop, hybrid memory/RAG,
  OSINT toolkit v2.0 (80+ platform username search), STT/TTS pipelines,
  plugin system, chat-history import from Claude/ChatGPT/Gemini exports.
- **v6** — Config adapter dotted-key lookup bug fixed; config-cache
  invalidation on reconfigure so subsystems pick up changes without restart.
- **v4–v5** — Six confirmed architectural bugs fixed (init-order
  `AttributeError`s, etc.); neural network canvas animation; multi-theme
  system; task queue; deep research panel; model manager; settings dialog
  with multiple tabs.
- **JARVIS v1–v4.2** — Original architecture: PySide6/PyQt UI, Ollama backend,
  ChromaDB semantic memory, RAG, tool/agent system, STT/TTS, browser control
  via CDP, plugin system. LangChain / LlamaIndex / Open Interpreter
  integrations explored.

---

## How to tag this release

```bash
git add -A
git commit -m "Aether v1.0.0 — rebrand + ethereal UI redesign"
git tag -a v1.0.0 -m "Aether v1.0.0"
git push origin main --tags
```

If you'd rather ship this as a `v0.5.0` (pre-1.0, since wake word / tabbed
settings / full test pass are still open), just swap the version string above —
nothing in the code depends on the tag itself.

================================================================================
FILE: config_validator.py

"""
core/config_validator.py — Aether Configuration Validator

Called at startup before any subsystem loads.
Catches missing keys, bad paths, invalid engine names, and unreachable URLs
so you get a clear error message instead of a cryptic crash later.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import logging
import os
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


@dataclass
class ValidationIssue:
    level:   str   # "error" | "warning" | "info"
    key:     str
    message: str


@dataclass
class ValidationResult:
    issues: list[ValidationIssue] = field(default_factory=list)

    @property
    def errors(self)   -> list[ValidationIssue]:
        return [i for i in self.issues if i.level == "error"]

    @property
    def warnings(self) -> list[ValidationIssue]:
        return [i for i in self.issues if i.level == "warning"]

    @property
    def ok(self) -> bool:
        return len(self.errors) == 0

    def add(self, level: str, key: str, msg: str) -> None:
        self.issues.append(ValidationIssue(level, key, msg))

    def format(self) -> str:
        if not self.issues:
            return "✅ Configuration valid."
        lines = ["Configuration validation:"]
        for i in self.issues:
            icon = {"error": "❌", "warning": "⚠️", "info": "ℹ️"}.get(i.level, "?")
            lines.append(f"  {icon}  [{i.key}]  {i.message}")
        return "\n".join(lines)


# ── Validators ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

_VALID_STT   = {"whisper", "vosk", "faster_whisper"}
_VALID_TTS   = {"edgetts", "kokoro", "elevenlabs", "edge_tts", "pyttsx3"}
_VALID_SAFE  = {"ask", "off", "auto"}


def _check_ollama_url(result: ValidationResult, cfg) -> None:
    # config uses "ollama_url" or "llm_url" — check both; "llm.ollama_url" is legacy
    url = (cfg.get("ollama_url", "")
           or cfg.get("llm_url", "")
           or cfg.get("llm.ollama_url", ""))
    if not url:
        result.add("error", "llm.ollama_url", "Missing — Ollama URL not set")
        return
    if not url.startswith("http"):
        result.add("error", "llm.ollama_url", f"Must start with http:// — got: {url}")
    try:
        import requests
        requests.get(f"{url}/api/tags", timeout=3)
    except Exception as e:
        result.add("warning", "llm.ollama_url",
            f"Could not reach Ollama at {url} — is it running? ({e})")


def _check_model(result: ValidationResult, cfg) -> None:
    model = cfg.get("llm.model", cfg.get("llm_model", ""))
    if not model:
        result.add("error", "llm.model",
            "No model configured — set llm_model in config/api_keys.json")
    elif " " in model:
        result.add("error", "llm.model",
            f"Model name contains spaces: '{model}' — use format like qwen3:8b")


def _check_stt(result: ValidationResult, cfg) -> None:
    engine = cfg.get("stt_engine", "whisper").lower().replace("-", "_")
    if engine not in _VALID_STT:
        result.add("warning", "stt_engine",
            f"Unknown STT engine '{engine}' — valid: {sorted(_VALID_STT)}")


def _check_tts(result: ValidationResult, cfg) -> None:
    engine = cfg.get("tts_engine", "edgetts").lower().replace("-", "_")
    normalized = {"edge_tts": "edgetts"}.get(engine, engine)
    if normalized not in _VALID_TTS:
        result.add("warning", "tts_engine",
            f"Unknown TTS engine '{engine}' — valid: {sorted(_VALID_TTS)}")


def _check_paths(result: ValidationResult, cfg) -> None:
    """Check that configured file paths are accessible."""
    path_keys = {
        "memory.db_path":   False,   # file — parent must exist or be creatable
        "rag.index_path":   False,
        "logging.log_dir":  False,
    }
    for key, must_exist in path_keys.items():
        val = cfg.get(key, "")
        if not val:
            continue
        p = Path(os.path.expanduser(val))
        if must_exist and not p.exists():
            result.add("error", key, f"Path does not exist: {p}")
        else:
            # Try creating parent
            try:
                p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
            except Exception as e:
                result.add("warning", key, f"Cannot create directory: {e}")


def _check_oi_safe_mode(result: ValidationResult, cfg) -> None:
    mode = cfg.get("oi.safe_mode", "ask")
    if mode not in _VALID_SAFE:
        result.add("warning", "oi.safe_mode",
            f"Unknown safe_mode '{mode}' — valid: ask | off | auto")


def _check_mcp(result: ValidationResult, cfg) -> None:
    if not cfg.get("mcp.enabled", False):
        return
    servers = cfg.get("mcp.servers", [])
    if not servers:
        result.add("info", "mcp.enabled",
            "MCP enabled but no servers configured in mcp.servers")
    for i, srv in enumerate(servers):
        if not srv.get("name"):
            result.add("error", f"mcp.servers[{i}]", "Missing 'name' field")
        if not srv.get("command"):
            result.add("error", f"mcp.servers[{i}]", "Missing 'command' field")


def _check_memory(result: ValidationResult, cfg) -> None:
    limit = cfg.get("memory.chat_history_limit", 100)
    try:
        if int(limit) < 10:
            result.add("warning", "memory.chat_history_limit",
                f"Value {limit} is very low — may cause poor recall")
    except (ValueError, TypeError):
        result.add("error", "memory.chat_history_limit",
            f"Must be an integer — got: {limit!r}")

    threshold = cfg.get("memory.relevance_threshold", 0.35)
    try:
        t = float(threshold)
        if not 0.0 <= t <= 1.0:
            result.add("warning", "memory.relevance_threshold",
                f"Should be 0.0–1.0 — got: {t}")
    except (ValueError, TypeError):
        result.add("error", "memory.relevance_threshold",
            f"Must be a float — got: {threshold!r}")


# ── Public API ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def validate_config(config=None) -> ValidationResult:
    """
    Validate the full configuration.
    Call this at startup before loading any subsystem.
    """
    from memory.config_manager import get_mark_config
    cfg = config or get_mark_config()
    result = ValidationResult()

    _check_ollama_url(result, cfg)
    _check_model(result, cfg)
    _check_stt(result, cfg)
    _check_tts(result, cfg)
    _check_paths(result, cfg)
    _check_oi_safe_mode(result, cfg)
    _check_mcp(result, cfg)
    _check_memory(result, cfg)

    if result.ok and not result.warnings:
        result.add("info", "all", "All checks passed ✓")

    return result


if __name__ == "__main__":
    r = validate_config()
    print(r.format())
    if not r.ok:
        import sys
        sys.exit(1)


================================================================================
FILE: crash_logger.py
================================================================================

"""
core/crash_logger.py — Aether Crash Recovery Logging

Writes structured crash reports to logs/crashes/ whenever an unhandled
exception occurs during tool execution or message processing.

Each crash file is a JSON document containing:
  - timestamp + human-readable date
  - exception type, message, and full traceback
  - the user message that triggered it
  - the tool being executed (if any)
  - system context (Python version, platform, memory usage)

Usage:
    from core.crash_logger import log_crash, get_recent_crashes

    try:
        ...
    except Exception as e:
        log_crash(e, user_message="What time is it?", tool="reminder")

Read recent crashes in chat: "show recent crashes" / "crash report"
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import logging
import os
import platform
import sys
import time
import traceback
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

_CRASH_DIR = Path("logs/crashes")
_MAX_CRASH_FILES = 50   # rotate when this many exist


def _system_context() -> dict:
    ctx: dict[str, Any] = {
        "python": sys.version,
        "platform": platform.platform(),
        "cwd": str(Path.cwd()),
    }
    try:
        import psutil
        vm = psutil.virtual_memory()
        ctx["ram_used_mb"]  = vm.used  // (1024 * 1024)
        ctx["ram_total_mb"] = vm.total // (1024 * 1024)
        ctx["ram_pct"]      = vm.percent
        ctx["cpu_pct"]      = psutil.cpu_percent(interval=0.1)
    except ImportError:
        pass
    return ctx


def log_crash(exc: BaseException,
              user_message: str = "",
              tool: str = "",
              extra: dict | None = None) -> str:
    """
    Write a crash report to logs/crashes/<timestamp>.json.
    Returns the path to the crash file (for display to the user).
    """
    try:
        _CRASH_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

        # Rotate old crash files
        existing = sorted(_CRASH_DIR.glob("*.json"))
        while len(existing) >= _MAX_CRASH_FILES:
            existing.pop(0).unlink(missing_ok=True)

        stamp = datetime.now().strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S_%f")[:20]
        path  = _CRASH_DIR / f"crash_{stamp}.json"

        report = {
            "timestamp":    time.time(),
            "date":         datetime.now().isoformat(),
            "exception":    type(exc).__name__,
            "message":      str(exc),
            "traceback":    traceback.format_exc(),
            "user_message": user_message[:500] if user_message else "",
            "tool":         tool,
            "system":       _system_context(),
            "extra":        extra or {},
        }

        path.write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2, default=str),
                        encoding="utf-8")
        logger.debug("Crash logged: %s", path)
        return str(path)

    except Exception as write_err:
        logger.error("Failed to write crash log: %s", write_err)
        return ""


def get_recent_crashes(n: int = 5) -> str:
    """Return a human-readable summary of the n most recent crashes."""
    try:
        if not _CRASH_DIR.exists():
            return "No crash logs found."

        files = sorted(_CRASH_DIR.glob("*.json"))[-n:]
        if not files:
            return "No crash logs found."

        lines = [f"## Recent Crashes ({len(files)} of {len(list(_CRASH_DIR.glob('*.json')))} total)", ""]
        for f in reversed(files):
            try:
                data = json.loads(f.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
                date = data.get("date", "?")[:19]
                exc  = data.get("exception", "?")
                msg  = data.get("message",   "?")[:100]
                tool = data.get("tool", "")
                user = data.get("user_message", "")[:80]
                lines.append(f"**{date}**  `{exc}`: {msg}")
                if tool:
                    lines.append(f"  Tool: `{tool}`")
                if user:
                    lines.append(f"  Trigger: \"{user}\"")
                lines.append("")
            except Exception:
                lines.append(f"{f.name}: could not read")

        lines.append(f"Crash files at: {_CRASH_DIR}/")
        return "\n".join(lines)
    except Exception as e:
        return f"Could not read crash logs: {e}"


def crash_count() -> int:
    """Return total number of crash files."""
    if not _CRASH_DIR.exists():
        return 0
    return len(list(_CRASH_DIR.glob("*.json")))

FILE: dump.py
================================================================================

"""
dump.py — MARK XL Codebase Exporter
Generates a single text file containing all relevant source files.
Perfect for sharing with AI assistants or for code reviews.
"""

import os
from pathlib import Path

# ── Config ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# Folders to skip entirely (to avoid bloating the dump)
IGNORE_LIST = {
    '.git', '__pycache__', '.venv', 'venv', 'node_modules',
    'data', 'logs', '.pytest_cache', '.mypy_cache', '.ruff_cache'
}

# File extensions to include
ALLOWED_EXTENSIONS = {'.py', '.md', '.json', '.txt', '.yaml', '.toml', '.ini'}

# Optional: only include files from these subdirectories
# Set to None to include everything (except ignored folders)
INCLUDE_SUBDIRS = None  # e.g., ['core', 'actions', 'agent', 'memory', 'integrations']

# Output file name
OUTPUT_FILE = 'code_dump.txt'


def dump_codebase(root_dir: str, output_file: str) -> None:
    """
    Walk the project directory and write all matching files into a single text file.
    """
    root = Path(root_dir).resolve()
    if not root.exists():
        print(f"❌ Root directory not found: {root}")
        return

    print(f"📂 Scanning: {root}")
    file_count = 0

    with open(output_file, 'w', encoding='utf-8') as out:
        # Iterate over all files recursively
        for path in root.rglob('*'):
            # Skip ignored directories (by checking path parts)
            if any(part in IGNORE_LIST for part in path.parts):
                continue

            # Restrict to specific subdirs if configured
            if INCLUDE_SUBDIRS is not None:
                rel = path.relative_to(root)
                if not any(str(rel).startswith(d) for d in INCLUDE_SUBDIRS):
                    continue

            # Only process files with allowed extensions
            if path.is_file() and path.suffix in ALLOWED_EXTENSIONS:
                relative_path = path.relative_to(root)
                out.write(f"\n\n{'='*80}\n")
                out.write(f"FILE: {relative_path}\n")
                out.write(f"{'='*80}\n\n")
                try:
                    content = path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')
                    out.write(content)
                    file_count += 1
                except UnicodeDecodeError:
                    out.write(f"[SKIPPED: binary or non-UTF-8 file]")
                except Exception as e:
                    out.write(f"[COULD NOT READ FILE: {e}]")

    print(f"✅ Done! Exported {file_count} file(s) to: {output_file}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    dump_codebase('.', OUTPUT_FILE)
    print("\n💡 Tip: If the file is too large, edit INCLUDE_SUBDIRS at the top to export only specific folders.")

================================================================================
FILE: evaluator.py
================================================================================

"""
core/evaluator.py — Aether Self-Evaluation System
Inspired by OpenAether evaluation architecture.

The evaluator asks the LLM: "Did the response actually solve the user's request?"
If not, it returns a structured failure report so the agent can retry or replan.

This is the #1 thing that separates a demo bot from a real assistant.
Silent failures get caught instead of being silently swallowed.

Usage:
    from core.evaluator import Evaluator
    ev = Evaluator()
    result = ev.evaluate(user_request, assistant_response, tool_results)
    if not result.passed:
        print(f"Failed: {result.reason}")
        # trigger replan or retry
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import logging
import re
import json
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


@dataclass
class EvalResult:
    passed: bool
    score: float          # 0.0 – 1.0
    reason: str = ""
    suggestions: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    needs_retry: bool = False
    needs_replan: bool = False


_EVAL_SYSTEM = """You are a strict quality-control evaluator for an AI assistant.

Given:
1. The user's original request
2. The assistant's final response
3. Any tool results that were produced

Your job: assess whether the assistant ACTUALLY completed the user's request.

Score 0.0–1.0:
  1.0 = fully completed, correct, actionable
  0.7 = mostly done, minor gaps
  0.4 = partial, important parts missing
  0.0 = failed, wrong, or refused

Respond with ONLY valid JSON:
{
  "passed": true|false,
  "score": 0.0-1.0,
  "reason": "one sentence explaining your verdict",
  "suggestions": ["what could be improved"],
  "needs_retry": false,
  "needs_replan": false
}

passed = score >= 0.6
needs_retry = transient failure (network, timeout) — same approach could work
needs_replan = wrong approach — different tools/strategy needed"""


class Evaluator:
    """
    Evaluates whether a response actually solved the user's request.
    Gracefully degrades: if the LLM call fails, returns passed=True
    so execution isn't blocked.
    """

    def __init__(self, enabled: bool = True, threshold: float = 0.6) -> None:
        self.enabled = enabled
        self.threshold = threshold

    def evaluate(
        self,
        user_request: str,
        assistant_response: str,
        tool_results: list[tuple[str, str]] | None = None,
    ) -> EvalResult:
        if not self.enabled:
            return EvalResult(passed=True, score=1.0, reason="Evaluation disabled")

        tool_summary = ""
        if tool_results:
            lines = [f"  [{t}]: {r[:200]}" for t, r in tool_results[:5]]
            tool_summary = "Tool results:\n" + "\n".join(lines)

        prompt = (
            f"User request: {user_request}\n\n"
            f"Assistant response:\n{assistant_response[:600]}\n\n"
            f"{tool_summary}"
        )

        try:
            from core.llm_client import call_llm_text
            raw = call_llm_text(prompt, system=_EVAL_SYSTEM)
            raw = re.sub(r"```(?:json)?", "", raw).strip().rstrip("`").strip()
            data = json.loads(raw)
            score = float(data.get("score", 0.5))
            return EvalResult(
                passed=score >= self.threshold,
                score=score,
                reason=data.get("reason", ""),
                suggestions=data.get("suggestions", []),
                needs_retry=bool(data.get("needs_retry", False)),
                needs_replan=bool(data.get("needs_replan", False)),
            )
        except Exception as e:
            logger.debug("Evaluator failed (non-fatal): %s", e)
            # Graceful degradation: don't block on eval failure
            return EvalResult(passed=True, score=0.5, reason=f"Eval unavailable: {e}")

    def quick_check(self, response: str) -> bool:
        """
        Fast heuristic check without an LLM call.
        Returns False if the response looks like an obvious failure.
        """
        lower = response.lower()
        failure_phrases = [
            "i cannot", "i can't", "i'm unable", "i don't have access",
            "i apologize", "error occurred", "something went wrong",
            "failed to", "could not complete", "not able to",
        ]
        return not any(p in lower for p in failure_phrases)


================================================================================
FILE: file_watcher.py
================================================================================

"""
core/file_watcher.py — Aether File Watcher
Auto-indexes new files dropped into watched directories into the RAG engine.
Inspired by OpenAether connectors/sync_engine.py pattern.

Usage:
    from core.file_watcher import FileWatcher
    watcher = FileWatcher(rag_engine, watched_dirs=["~/Documents", "~/Obsidian"])
    watcher.start()   # non-blocking, runs in background thread
    watcher.stop()
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import logging
import os
import threading
import time
from pathlib import Path

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

_SUPPORTED = {".pdf", ".md", ".txt", ".rst", ".csv", ".docx"}


class FileWatcher:
    """
    Watches directories for new/modified files and auto-indexes them into RAG.
    Uses polling (no watchdog dependency) for maximum compatibility.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        rag_engine,
        watched_dirs: list[str] | None = None,
        poll_interval: float = 5.0,
        notify_cb=None,
    ) -> None:
        self._rag = rag_engine
        self._dirs = [Path(os.path.expanduser(d)) for d in (watched_dirs or [])]
        self._interval = poll_interval
        self._notify_cb = notify_cb  # optional: fn(str) to show a log message
        self._seen: dict[str, float] = {}   # path → mtime
        self._running = False
        self._thread: threading.Thread | None = None

    def start(self) -> None:
        if not self._dirs:
            logger.debug("FileWatcher: no directories configured, not starting")
            return
        self._running = True
        self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._run, daemon=True)
        self._thread.start()
        logger.info("FileWatcher started — watching %d dir(s)", len(self._dirs))

    def stop(self) -> None:
        self._running = False

    def add_directory(self, path: str) -> None:
        p = Path(os.path.expanduser(path))
        if p not in self._dirs:
            self._dirs.append(p)
            logger.info("FileWatcher: added %s", p)

    def _run(self) -> None:
        while self._running:
            try:
                self._scan()
            except Exception as e:
                logger.debug("FileWatcher scan error: %s", e)
            time.sleep(self._interval)

    def _scan(self) -> None:
        for watch_dir in self._dirs:
            if not watch_dir.exists():
                continue
            for root, _, files in os.walk(watch_dir):
                for fname in files:
                    fpath = Path(root) / fname
                    if fpath.suffix.lower() not in _SUPPORTED:
                        continue
                    try:
                        mtime = fpath.stat().st_mtime
                        key   = str(fpath)
                        prev_mtime = self._seen.get(key)
                        self._seen[key] = mtime   # always update seen record
                        if prev_mtime is None:
                            # First time we've seen this file — just record it,
                            # don't index everything on startup
                            pass
                        elif prev_mtime < mtime:
                            # File was modified since last scan — index it
                            self._index(fpath)
                    except OSError:
                        pass

    def _index(self, path: Path) -> None:
        if self._rag is None:
            return
        try:
            result = self._rag.index_file(str(path))
            msg = f"FileWatcher: auto-indexed {path.name} → {result}"
            logger.info(msg)
            if self._notify_cb:
                self._notify_cb(msg)
        except Exception as e:
            logger.debug("FileWatcher index error for %s: %s", path.name, e)


def start_file_watcher(rag_engine, config=None, notify_cb=None) -> FileWatcher | None:
    """
    Convenience: create and start a FileWatcher from config.
    Returns the watcher instance (or None if no dirs configured).
    """
    watch_dirs: list[str] = []
    if config:
        try:
            watch_dirs = config.get("rag.watch_dirs", []) or []
        except Exception:
            pass

    if not watch_dirs:
        return None

    watcher = FileWatcher(rag_engine, watched_dirs=watch_dirs, notify_cb=notify_cb)
    watcher.start()
    return watcher

# Aether — Improved Edition
## What Was Added (from Aether v4.2)

This document describes every module integrated from `certifiedclaw/aether-v4.2`
and exactly where each file lives in the new project structure.

---

## New Files Added

| File | Source | What It Does |
|------|--------|--------------|
| `memory/semantic_memory.py` | `memory_engine.py` | SQLite-backed episodic memory with sentence-transformer embeddings. Stores every exchange and retrieves relevant past context for each new message. |
| `memory/rag.py` | `rag.py` | Local RAG engine — index your Obsidian vault, PDF folders, or any `.md/.txt/.csv` files. Query them by asking Aether. |
| `core/smart_context.py` | `smart_context.py` | Auto-compresses conversation history when it approaches the LLM token limit. Keeps system messages, drops oldest turns first. |
| `core/plugins.py` | `plugins.py` | Drop-in plugin system. Place any `.py` file in `plugins/` with `PLUGIN_NAME` and `PLUGIN_TOOLS` defined — Aether auto-loads it at startup. |
| `core/plugin_watcher.py` | `plugin_watcher.py` | Watches `plugins/` with `watchdog`. Reloads plugins automatically when you add/edit/delete a file — no restart needed. |
| `core/task_scheduler.py` | `task_scheduler.py` | Background task scheduler. Register recurring tasks (e.g. "summarise news every hour"). Also exposed as an LLM tool. |
| `core/hotkey_manager.py` | `hotkey_commands.py` | Global hotkey (`Ctrl+Alt+J`) to show/hide the window from anywhere. Requires `keyboard` package. |
| `core/safety.py` | `safety.py` | Path ACL + high-risk tool confirmation layer. `run_shell` / `shell` are always blocked. Destructive tools (`delete`, `write_file`) require confirmation. |
| `core/mcp_bridge.py` | `mcp_bridge.py` | MCP (Model Context Protocol) bridge. Connect external MCP servers (Obsidian, GitHub, Postgres, etc.) via config. No restart needed. |
| `core/diagnostics.py` | `diagnostics.py` | Full system health check: Python version, Ollama status, GPU, all packages, browser CDP. Type `status` in chat to run. |
| `actions/osint_tools.py` | `osint_tools.py` | OSINT toolkit: WHOIS, DNS, subdomain enum, email breach check, port scan, Google dorks, IP geolocation, username search, SSL cert info, tech stack detection. |
| `actions/pdf_tools.py` | `pdf_tools.py` | PDF utilities: extract text, summarise, extract tables, search within document. Requires `pip install pymupdf`. |
| `actions/extra_tools.py` | `extra_tools.py` | Expanded utility tools from Aether (clipboard manager, process list, network info, etc.) |

---

## Changes to Existing Files

### `main.py` — Fully Rewritten
- All original tool dispatching preserved exactly
- Added imports for all new modules
- `_build_system_prompt()` now injects semantic memory context + MCP/plugin tool lists
- `_process_message()` now:
  - Handles `/clear` and `status` commands
  - Stores exchanges to semantic memory
  - Runs `compress_if_needed()` on history before each LLM call
  - Appends new tools: `osint_lookup`, `pdf_tool`, `rag_query`, `diagnostics`
  - Falls through to plugin tools if tool name not found in built-ins
  - Falls through to MCP bridge for `mcp.call`
- `_execute_tool()` extended with 5 new tool handlers
- New `_init_new_subsystems()` method — runs in background thread at startup
- All new subsystems start in parallel, non-blocking

### `memory/config_manager.py` — Extended
- Added `_MarkConfigAdapter` class with `.get("dotted.key")` interface
- Added `get_mark_config()` singleton — bridges Mark-XL's JSON config to the
  dict-style API that all Aether-borrowed modules expect

---

## New Tool Declarations (callable by the LLM)

| Tool | Trigger phrases |
|------|----------------|
| `osint_lookup` | "who owns domain X", "check if email was breached", "open ports on Y", "DNS records for Z" |
| `pdf_tool` | "summarize this PDF", "extract text from file.pdf", "search my PDF for X" |
| `rag_query` | "search my notes about X", "index this folder", "what does my Obsidian say about Y" |
| `diagnostics` | "status", "system check", "health check", "aether status" |

---

## New Chat Commands

| Command | Action |
|---------|--------|
| `status` | Run full system diagnostics |
| `/clear` | Clear chat history and semantic memory session |

---

## New Runtime Directories

```
Mark-XL/
├── plugins/          ← drop .py plugin files here (hot-reloaded)
├── data/
│   ├── memory.db     ← semantic memory (auto-created)
│   └── rag_index/    ← RAG document index (auto-created)
└── logs/
    └── aether.log    ← rotating log, 5 MB cap
```

---

## Quick Setup

```bash
# Install new dependencies
pip install sentence-transformers watchdog keyboard PyMuPDF python-whois dnspython

# Run as normal — all new systems initialise in the background
python main.py
```

---

## Writing a Plugin

Drop a `.py` file in `plugins/`:

```python
PLUGIN_NAME = "my_tool"

def greet(name: str = "world") -> str:
    return f"Hello, {name}!"

PLUGIN_TOOLS = {
    "greet": greet,
}
```

Aether will pick it up instantly (no restart). Ask it: *"use my_tool to greet Alice"*.

---

## Configuring MCP Servers

Add to `config/api_keys.json`:

```json
{
  "mcp_enabled": true,
  "mcp_servers": [
    {
      "name": "obsidian",
      "command": "npx",
      "args": ["-y", "@modelcontextprotocol/server-obsidian", "/path/to/vault"]
    }
  ]
}
```

Then ask: *"search my Obsidian notes about project X"*.

# Aether — v2 Upgrade Notes

## Six gaps addressed

### 1. Memory quality — `memory/hybrid_memory.py`
Replaced pure-dense retrieval with **three-stage hybrid**:
- **Stage 1 BM25** — fast keyword scoring, zero extra deps, catches exact names/commands
- **Stage 2 Dense** — sentence-transformer semantic similarity
- **Stage 3 Cross-encoder reranker** — re-scores top candidates for final precision (optional, falls back gracefully)

Upgrade path: `HybridMemory` is API-compatible with the old `MemoryEngine` — `main.py` uses it automatically when available and falls back to `MemoryEngine` otherwise.

Upgraded `memory/rag.py` uses the same BM25+dense hybrid for document retrieval (previously pure-dense).

---

### 2. Evaluation — `evaluator.py` (already existed, now wired in)
The evaluator existed in the zip but was never called. It is now invoked at the end of every `_process_message` turn that produced tool results.

On failure (score < 0.6):
- `obs.count("eval.failed")` is recorded
- `obs.log("eval_fail", ...)` emits a structured event to `logs/events.jsonl`
- A warning is written to the UI log

---

### 3. State management — `core/goal_state.py`
New file. Persistent goals survive session restarts:
- Goals stored as JSON to `data/goals.json`
- Injected into system prompt so the LLM always knows active goals
- LLM can create/update/complete/cancel goals via `goal_manager` tool
- Priority queue: urgent → high → normal → low
- Sub-tasks per goal with completion tracking

---

### 4. Autonomy — `core/task_scheduler.py` (already existed, now enhanced)
No changes needed — already wired. File watcher adds autonomous background indexing: drop a file into a watched directory and it's indexed into RAG automatically.

---

### 5. Permissions — `core/safety.py` (already existed)
No changes. The existing v2 safety layer is correct — `run_shell` always requires confirmation, path ACL enforced.

---

### 6. Observability — `core/observability.py`
New file. Three components:
- **StructuredLogger** — writes `logs/events.jsonl` (newline-delimited JSON, parseable by any log aggregator)
- **MetricsCollector** — in-memory counters and timing histograms (`tool.calls`, `eval.passed`, `loop_guard.blocked`, etc.)
- **Tracer** — lightweight span tracing around every tool call; optional OpenTelemetry export if SDK installed

Access the report in chat: type `obs report` (routes to `obs_report` tool).

---

## New files

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `memory/hybrid_memory.py` | BM25 + dense + cross-encoder retrieval |
| `core/observability.py`   | Structured logs, metrics, spans |
| `core/goal_state.py`      | Persistent goal management |
| `memory/rag.py`           | Upgraded to hybrid BM25+dense (replaces pure-dense) |

## Changed files

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| `main.py` | Loop guard reset per request; observability spans around tool calls; evaluator called after each turn; hybrid memory preferred over plain semantic memory; goal state injected into system prompt; goal_manager + obs_report tool handlers added; all v2 systems started in `_init_new_subsystems()` |
| `requirements.txt` | Added `python-docx` for .docx RAG indexing |

## New tool available to the LLM

| Tool | Trigger phrases |
|------|----------------|
| `goal_manager` | "remember my goal to...", "track project X", "what are my active goals", "mark goal abc123 done" |
| `obs_report` | "show metrics", "observability report", "how many tool calls today" |

# Aether — v3 Improvements

## What's new in v3

### integrations/ — new module

Three external frameworks integrated as optional library dependencies.
All are lazy-loaded with graceful fallback messages if not installed.

---

### 1. LangChain (`integrations/langchain_tools.py`)
`pip install langchain langchain-community langchain-ollama duckduckgo-search wikipedia`

| Action | What it does |
|--------|-------------|
| `web_search` | DuckDuckGo search — no API key needed |
| `wikipedia` | Wikipedia lookup |
| `summarize` | Map-reduce summarisation for arbitrarily long documents |
| `research` | ReAct multi-hop agent — searches web iteratively to answer complex questions |
| `load_doc` | Load PDF / DOCX / CSV / HTML / MD into text for RAG indexing |

**Also wired:** `lc_compress_history` now replaces the basic `compress_if_needed`
in the main conversation loop. Old turns are summarised by the LLM instead of
being dropped, so Aether retains the meaning of long conversations within the
token budget.

---

### 2. Open Interpreter (`integrations/oi_shell.py`)
`pip install open-interpreter`

Natural language computer control. The LLM writes Python/shell/JS code and
executes it locally to accomplish tasks that can't be done with fixed tools:
- Process and plot data files
- Batch-rename / move / zip files
- Install packages and configure software
- Automate anything the shell can do

| Action | What it does |
|--------|-------------|
| `run` | Run a single task (isolated) |
| `chat` | Multi-turn session (remembers variables, files) |
| `reset` | Clear session state |
| `status` | Show current config |

**Safe mode:** defaults to `"ask"` — OI confirms before executing.
Set `oi.safe_mode: "off"` in config.yaml for fully autonomous mode.
Uses the same Ollama model and URL as the rest of Aether.

---

### 3. LlamaIndex (`integrations/llama_index_rag.py`)
`pip install llama-index llama-index-llms-ollama llama-index-embeddings-ollama`

Production-grade RAG. Better than the built-in RAG engine for:
- Large document collections
- Complex multi-part questions (SubQuestion decomposition)
- Mixed document types in one index

| Action | What it does |
|--------|-------------|
| `index` | Index a file or directory (PDF, DOCX, CSV, HTML, MD, TXT) |
| `query` | Search the index (mode: auto / vector / summary / sub) |
| `summarize` | Summarise an entire folder with tree_summarize |
| `collections` | List all named indexes |

Optional: add `chromadb` + `llama-index-vector-stores-chroma` for persistent
indexes that survive restarts.

---

## Tool routing cheat-sheet

| Task | Tool |
|------|------|
| Quick web question | `langchain(action=web_search)` |
| Multi-step research | `langchain(action=research)` |
| Summarise a long doc | `langchain(action=summarize)` or `llama_index(action=summarize)` |
| Run code / edit files | `open_interpreter(action=run)` |
| Search indexed documents | `llama_index(action=query)` |
| Add docs to knowledge base | `llama_index(action=index)` |

---

## Install all v3 integrations at once

```bash
pip install langchain langchain-community langchain-ollama \
            duckduckgo-search wikipedia \
            open-interpreter \
            llama-index llama-index-llms-ollama llama-index-embeddings-ollama
```

# Aether — v4 Improvements

## What's new in v4

### New top-level modules (previously unconnected, now wired in)

These files existed in the zip but were not imported or called from `main.py`.
v4 connects them all.

---

### 1. Crash Logger (`crash_logger.py`)

Writes a structured JSON report to `logs/crashes/` whenever any unhandled
exception occurs — either in tool execution or in the main message loop.

Each report includes:
- Exception type, message, and full traceback
- The user message that triggered it
- The tool being executed (if any)
- Python version, platform, and RAM/CPU usage at time of crash

**Wired in v4:**
- `_execute_tool` now calls `log_crash(e, tool=name, ...)` on any exception
- `_process_message` is now wrapped — any exception that escapes the inner
  loop is caught, logged, and surfaced to the user gracefully

**New chat commands:**
- `/crashes` — show the 5 most recent crash reports
- `"show crashes"` / `"crash report"` — same via natural language

---

### 2. Self-Test (`self_test.py`)

End-to-end system health check covering:
- Python version ≥ 3.10
- Ollama reachability
- LLM response (minimal prompt)
- SQLite memory DB
- Long-term memory read/write cycle
- Microphone and speaker device detection
- (If app is running) Hybrid memory, RAG engine, goals, plugins,
  evaluator, observability, loop guard

**Wired in v4:**
- `/selftest` chat command triggers `run_self_test(aether=self)`
- `"run self test"` / `"self test"` also trigger it
- Report printed to the log panel

---

### 3. Memory Tools (`memory_tools.py`)

Backup, export, import, prune, and stats for the memory system.

| Action | What it does |
|--------|-------------|
| `backup` | Zip snapshot of `memory.db`, `long_term.json`, `goals.json`, RAG index |
| `export` | Export DB memories to readable JSON |
| `import` | Restore from a previous export |
| `prune`  | Remove duplicates, too-short entries, and old entries from DB |
| `stats`  | Detailed stats across all memory systems |

**Wired in v4:**
- New `memory_tool` LLM tool declaration — the LLM can now call these
- Direct chat commands: `/backup`, `/memstats`, `/prune`, `"backup memory"`, etc.

---

### 4. Config Validator (`config_validator.py`)

Validates the full configuration before any subsystem loads.
Catches: missing keys, bad Ollama URL, unknown STT/TTS engines,
inaccessible paths, MCP config errors, bad memory thresholds.

**Wired in v4:**
- Called once in `main()` immediately after dependency install,
  before `AetherLocal` is constructed
- All issues (errors/warnings/info) are printed to the UI log
- Config errors are flagged clearly so users don't get cryptic crashes later

---

### 5. Two new LLM tools

| Tool | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `memory_tool` | Backup / export / import / prune / stats via voice or text |
| `crash_report` | Show recent crash logs via voice or text |

### 6. Evaluator — `quick_check` now used as pre-filter

`Evaluator.quick_check()` existed but was never called. It's now run before
the full LLM evaluation: if the response already looks like an obvious failure
(contains phrases like "I cannot", "error occurred", etc.) the fast heuristic
catches it immediately without spending an LLM call on evaluation.

---

### 7. `call_llm_text` — tier routing

Added `tier=` parameter (`"fast"` / `"deep"` / `"default"`).

- `"fast"` — 30s timeout, 300 tokens (classification, error analysis, quick decisions)
- `"deep"` — 180s timeout, 1200 tokens (code generation, planning, summarisation)
- `"default"` — 120s timeout, 600 tokens (everything else)

The top-level `executor.py`, `planner.py`, and `error_handler.py` were already
using `tier=` calls; now `call_llm_text` actually honours them.

---

### 8. Agent files merged

The top-level `executor.py`, `planner.py`, and `error_handler.py` (which had been
updated for v4 but left outside `agent/`) have been merged into `agent/`.
Improvements included: explicit `{step_N}` placeholder injection between plan steps,
generic auto-inject for any content-bearing parameter, `extra_tool` dispatch,
and corrected `generated_code` guard rule.

---

| File | Fix |
|------|-----|
| `self_test.py` | `Any` was used in `_timed()` return type but not imported — moved to top-level imports |
| `config_validator.py` | URL key lookup now checks `ollama_url` and `llm_url` first, matching what `llm_client.py` and `config.example.json` actually use |
| `core/safety.py` | Removed `"not found"` and `"⚠️"` from `verify_tool_result` failure phrases — both appear in valid success responses, causing false negatives |
| `config/config.example.json` | Added all missing keys: `stt_language`, `tts_voice`, `llm_provider`, `llm_url`, `memory.*`, `logging.*`, `rag.*`, `rag.watch_dirs` |
| `file_watcher.py` | Fixed inverted first-scan logic — files were being indexed on first discovery (flooding RAG at startup) instead of only when modified |
| `core/llm_client.py` | Added `tier=` parameter (`"fast"` / `"deep"` / `"default"`) with appropriate timeout and `num_predict` overrides |
| `agent/executor.py` | Merged from improved top-level v4 version: `tier=` calls, explicit `{step_N}` placeholder injection, generic auto-inject for any content-bearing param, `extra_tool` dispatch |
| `agent/planner.py` | Merged from improved top-level v4 version: corrected `generated_code` rule wording, `extra_tool` / `image_gen` / `email_tool` declarations |
| `agent/error_handler.py` | Merged from improved top-level v4 version: `tier=` calls |

---

## New chat commands (v4 total)

| Command | Action |
|---------|--------|
| `/selftest` or `"run self test"` | Run full system self-test |
| `/crashes` or `"show crashes"` | Show recent crash logs |
| `/backup` or `"backup memory"` | Snapshot memory to zip |
| `/memstats` or `"memory stats"` | Show memory system stats |
| `/prune` or `"prune memory"` | Remove duplicate/old memories |
| `/status` / `"status"` | (existing) Full system diagnostic |
| `/clear` | (existing) Clear chat history |

---

## New runtime directories

```
Mark-XL/
└── logs/
    └── crashes/    ← JSON crash reports (auto-created, rotates at 50 files)
```

---

## v4 — Round 2 fixes

### `run_extra_tool` — written and wired in

`actions/extra_tools.py` had 45 individual utility functions but no dispatch
entry point. `agent/executor.py` called `run_extra_tool(action, params)` which
didn't exist, causing a silent `ImportError` on any `extra_tool` plan step.

Written: a full `run_extra_tool` dispatch table mapping every action name the
planner uses to the correct function, including name-normalisation for aliases
(`wifi_list` → `list_wifi_networks`, `auto_type` → `type_text`, `qr_code` →
`generate_qr`, etc.) and the two clipboard actions (`clipboard_read`,
`clipboard_write`) that were declared in the planner but had no implementation.

Also added `extra_tool` to `TOOL_DECLARATIONS` in `main.py` so the LLM can
call it directly from the main conversation loop (not only from multi-step plans).

### `_NEEDS_LLM_ROUND` — `code_helper` and `dev_agent` added

These tools return raw generated code/output; without an LLM synthesis round
the response was being sent directly to the user as an unformatted dump.

### CRLF line endings — normalised to LF across all files

18 Python files and `core/prompt.txt` had Windows CRLF line endings, which
cause `SyntaxError` on some Linux/macOS shells when files are exec'd or piped.
All normalised to LF.

### `requirements.txt` — added `qrcode[pil]` and `wikipedia-api`

Both are used by `extra_tools.py` actions but were missing from the dependency list.

### Additional bug fixes

| File | Fix |
|------|-----|
| `file_watcher.py` | *(previous round)* First-scan logic was inverted — all files were indexed at startup |
| `_NEEDS_LLM_ROUND` | Added `code_helper`, `dev_agent` — raw output now goes through an LLM synthesis round before being spoken/displayed |

FILE: IMPROVEMENTS_V6.md

# Aether — v6 Fixes

---

## 🔴 Bug fixes

### 1. `_MarkConfigAdapter.get()` silently ignored all dotted config keys
The adapter's `get()` checked whether the first segment of the key (`"memory"`)
existed in the raw dict. Since the JSON file stores keys as
`"memory.chat_history_limit"` (a literal dotted string), the segment `"memory"`
was never found, so the method always fell through to the built-in defaults —
ignoring whatever the user actually configured.

Every subsystem that uses the config adapter was affected: `HybridMemory`,
`SemanticMemory`, `RAGEngine`, `SafetyLayer`, etc. For example, setting
`"memory.chat_history_limit": 50` in `api_keys.json` had zero effect.

**Fix:** Replaced the broken segment-check with a direct `if key in self._raw`
lookup. Raw config values now always win over defaults.

---

### 2. `_MarkConfigAdapter` singleton not invalidated on reconfigure
When the user changed settings via **⚙ Configure**, `_do_reconfigure()` updated
`self._config` (the raw dict) but the config_manager singleton
(`_mark_config_instance`) was still holding the old `_MarkConfigAdapter`.
All subsystems that called `get_mark_config()` (memory, RAG, plugins, safety)
continued using the pre-reconfigure values for the rest of the session.

**Fix:** Added `invalidate_config_cache()` to `config_manager.py` and called it
at the start of `_do_reconfigure()`.

---

### 3. `call_llm_text` always used the Ollama endpoint, ignoring `llm_provider`
The planner, executor, error handler, code helper, dev agent, evaluator, and
context compressor all use `call_llm_text` for internal text generation.
When `llm_provider` was set to `"openai"` (LM Studio, LocalAI, Jan, etc.),
every one of those internal calls still hit `http://…/api/chat` (the Ollama
endpoint) and failed.

**Fix:** `call_llm_text` now checks `get_llm_provider()` and routes to
`/v1/chat/completions` for OpenAI-compatible backends, mirroring what
`call_llm` and `call_llm_stream` already did.

---

### 4. `error_handler.generate_fix()` fallback used `"generated_code"` tool name
When fix generation failed, the fallback step was emitted with
`tool: "generated_code"` — a tool name that is explicitly blocked by the
planner and executor (it exists only as an internal code-path, not a
dispatchable tool). The executor fell through to the unknown-tool branch.

**Fix:** Changed the fallback to `tool: "code_helper"` with
`action: "run"`, which is a real dispatchable tool.

Also fixed the same wrong replacement in `planner.create_plan()` and
`planner.replan()`: both guards were replacing `generated_code` steps with
`web_search`. A `generated_code` step is a code execution task, not a search —
the correct replacement is `code_helper(action="run")`.

---

### 5. `HybridMemory.store()` rebuilt the BM25 index on every single write
After every exchange, `store()` called `self._bm25.build(self._doc_texts)`,
which iterates the entire document list and recomputes term frequencies and
document frequencies from scratch. With 500+ memories this was O(n) per
message turn.

**Fix:** `store()` now sets a `_bm25_dirty` flag instead of rebuilding.
`_retrieve()` checks the flag and rebuilds lazily on the first query after
new documents were added — typically once per turn at most, and not at all
on turns with no memory writes.

---

### 6. `warmup_model()` in `reconfigure()` called without system prompt
When the user changed the LLM model via Configure, `warmup_model()` was called
without a `system_prompt` argument, so Ollama's KV prefix cache was not primed
with the Aether protocol text. The first real request after a model change would
incur full prompt evaluation latency instead of the near-zero cached latency.

**Fix:** `_do_reconfigure()` now loads the static system prompt and passes it to
`warmup_model(system_prompt=...)`, matching what the startup warmup path does.

---

## 🟡 Quality / reliability

### 7. Agent task failures not crash-logged
When an `agent_task` step threw an unhandled exception, `task_queue._run_task()`
caught it, printed it, and set the task status to FAILED — but never wrote a
structured crash report to `logs/crashes/`.

**Fix:** The `except` block in `_run_task()` now calls `log_crash()` with the
task ID and goal for context, matching the coverage added for the main
message loop and STT threads in v4/v5.

---

### 8. `smart_context` summarisation was permanently disabled
`ENABLE_SUMMARIZE = False` was hard-coded and the `router` parameter was never
passed by any caller, meaning dropped turns were silently discarded with no
summary. In a long session the LLM would lose all context from early turns.

**Fix:** Rewrote `compress_if_needed()` to call `call_llm_text()` directly
(no router needed) and always produce a 1-2 sentence summary of the dropped
turns, injected as a system message. The `router` parameter is kept for API
compatibility but ignored.

---

## Files changed

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| `memory/config_manager.py` | Fix 1 — `get()` dotted-key lookup; Fix 2 — `invalidate_config_cache()` |
| `main.py` | Fix 2 — call `invalidate_config_cache()` in `_do_reconfigure()`; Fix 6 — pass system prompt to `warmup_model()` |
| `core/llm_client.py` | Fix 3 — `call_llm_text` OpenAI provider routing |
| `agent/error_handler.py` | Fix 4 — fallback tool `generated_code` → `code_helper` |
| `agent/planner.py` | Fix 4b — `generated_code` guard → `code_helper` in both `create_plan()` and `replan()` |
| `memory/hybrid_memory.py` | Fix 5 — lazy BM25 rebuild via `_bm25_dirty` flag |
| `agent/task_queue.py` | Fix 7 — crash-log agent task failures |
| `core/smart_context.py` | Fix 8 — enable summarisation of dropped turns |


================================================================================
FILE: loop_guard.py
================================================================================

"""
core/loop_guard.py — Aether Loop Guard
Ported from OpenAether agents/loop_guard.py, adapted for flat architecture.

Detects when the LLM is stuck calling the same tool with the same args in a loop
and breaks the cycle by injecting a hard stop or forcing a different approach.

Usage in main.py _process_message:
    from core.loop_guard import LoopGuard
    guard = LoopGuard(max_repeats=2, window=4)
    # in tool call loop:
    if guard.check(tool_name, tool_args):
        break  # loop detected, stop
    guard.record(tool_name, tool_args)
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import hashlib
import json
import logging
from collections import deque
from dataclasses import dataclass

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


@dataclass
class GuardVerdict:
    blocked: bool
    reason: str = ""


class LoopGuard:
    """
    Tracks recent (tool, args) pairs and blocks if the same call
    repeats too many times within a rolling window.
    """

    def __init__(self, max_repeats: int = 2, window: int = 6) -> None:
        self._max_repeats = max_repeats
        self._window = window
        self._history: deque[str] = deque(maxlen=window)

    def _fingerprint(self, tool: str, args: dict | str) -> str:
        if isinstance(args, str):
            try:
                args = json.loads(args)
            except Exception:
                pass
        payload = json.dumps({"t": tool, "a": args}, sort_keys=True, default=str)
        return hashlib.md5(payload.encode()).hexdigest()[:12]

    def check(self, tool: str, args: dict | str) -> GuardVerdict:
        """
        Call BEFORE executing a tool.
        Returns GuardVerdict(blocked=True) if loop detected.
        """
        fp = self._fingerprint(tool, args)
        count = sum(1 for h in self._history if h == fp)
        if count >= self._max_repeats:
            reason = (
                f"Tool '{tool}' called with identical arguments "
                f"{count + 1} times in the last {self._window} calls. "
                "Breaking loop."
            )
            logger.warning("[LoopGuard] %s", reason)
            return GuardVerdict(blocked=True, reason=reason)
        return GuardVerdict(blocked=False)

    def record(self, tool: str, args: dict | str) -> None:
        """Call AFTER executing a tool to update history."""
        self._history.append(self._fingerprint(tool, args))

    def reset(self) -> None:
        """Reset between top-level user requests."""
        self._history.clear()

    def inject_break_message(self, tool: str) -> str:
        """Return a tool result that tells the LLM to stop looping."""
        return (
            f"[LOOP GUARD] Repeated call to '{tool}' blocked. "
            "You are in a loop. Stop calling this tool. "
            "Synthesize a final answer from what you already have, "
            "or tell the user you cannot complete this task."
        )

Aether — Improved Edition

# ── Bootstrap: auto-install base UI packages before anything else ──────────
import importlib.util as _ilu
import subprocess      as _sp
import sys             as _sys

_BASE_PKGS = [
    ("PyQt6",       "PyQt6"),
    ("psutil",      "psutil"),
    ("numpy",       "numpy"),
    ("sounddevice", "sounddevice"),
    ("PIL",         "pillow"),
    ("requests",    "requests"),
]

def _bootstrap() -> None:
    need = [pkg for mod, pkg in _BASE_PKGS if _ilu.find_spec(mod) is None]
    if not need:
        return
    print(f"\n[Aether] First-run setup — installing: {', '.join(need)}")
    print("[Aether] This happens only once.\n")
    _sp.run([_sys.executable, "-m", "pip", "install", *need], check=True)
    print("\n[Aether] Base packages ready — restarting…\n")
    _os.execv(_sys.executable, [_sys.executable] + _sys.argv)

_bootstrap()
